```
1	/**
2	 * Bill Matcher — maps vendor bill line items to canonical products.
3	 *
4	 * Multi-step matching pipeline:
5	 *   1. Exact name match against canonical_products
6	 *   2. Product alias cache hit (raw_name + optional HSN context)
7	 *   3. HSN-scoped fuzzy match (Fuse.js within same HSN category)
8	 *   4. LLM normalization (existing normalizer.ts)
9	 *   5. Unresolved → admin queue
10	 *
11	 * Each successful match learns an alias for future free lookups.
12	 * Over time, the alias cache grows and the LLM is called less.
13	 */
14	
15	import { nanoid } from "nanoid";
16	import type { BillLineItem, ExtractedBill } from "./bill-extractor";
17	import {
18	  createOrderFromBill,
19	  createOrderItemFromBill,
20	  findCanonicalProductByName,
21	  findProductAlias,
22	  insertUnresolvedBillItem,
23	  listCanonicalProductsByHsn,
24	  saveProductAlias,
25	  upsertCanonicalProduct,
26	  type CanonicalProduct,
27	} from "./db";
28	import { normalizeProductName } from "./normalizer";
29	
30	export type MatchResult = {
31	  matched: boolean;
32	  productId: string | null;
33	  canonicalName: string;
34	  confidence: number;
35	  method: "exact" | "alias" | "hsn_fuzzy" | "normalized" | "unresolved";
36	};
37	
38	/**
39	 * Simple fuzzy name comparison. Returns a similarity score 0-1.
40	 * Avoids pulling in Fuse.js server-side for a simple use case.
41	 */
42	function fuzzyScore(a: string, b: string): number {
43	  const la = a.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]/g, "");
44	  const lb = b.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]/g, "");
45	  if (la === lb) return 1.0;
46	  if (la.includes(lb) || lb.includes(la)) return 0.8;
47	
48	  // Bigram similarity (Dice coefficient)
49	  const bigramsA = new Set<string>();
50	  for (let i = 0; i < la.length - 1; i++) bigramsA.add(la.slice(i, i + 2));
51	  const bigramsB = new Set<string>();
52	  for (let i = 0; i < lb.length - 1; i++) bigramsB.add(lb.slice(i, i + 2));
53	
54	  if (bigramsA.size === 0 || bigramsB.size === 0) return 0;
55	
56	  let intersection = 0;
57	  for (const bg of bigramsA) {
58	    if (bigramsB.has(bg)) intersection++;
59	  }
60	  return (2 * intersection) / (bigramsA.size + bigramsB.size);
61	}
62	
63	export type Suggestion = {
64	  productId: string;
65	  canonicalName: string;
66	  unit: string;
67	  packSize: number;
68	  hsnCode: string | null;
69	  category: string | null;
70	  score: number;
71	  method: "exact" | "alias" | "hsn_fuzzy" | "normalized" | "global_fuzzy";
72	};
73	
74	/**
75	 * Non-mutating: returns the top-N catalog suggestions for a bill line.
76	 * Does NOT write aliases or learn anything — used by the admin review
77	 * UI so the user makes the final call.
78	 */
79	export async function suggestForBillLine(
80	  siteId: string,
81	  item: BillLineItem,
82	  topN = 3,
83	): Promise<Suggestion[]> {
84	  const name = item.item_purchased.trim();
85	  const hsn = item.hsn_code?.trim() || null;
86	  const pool = new Map<string, Suggestion>();
87	  const add = (s: Suggestion) => {
88	    const cur = pool.get(s.productId);
89	    if (!cur || s.score > cur.score) pool.set(s.productId, s);
90	  };
91	
92	  // Exact name → top suggestion
93	  const exact = await findCanonicalProductByName(siteId, name);
94	  if (exact) {
95	    add({
96	      productId: exact.product_id,
97	      canonicalName: exact.canonical_name,
98	      unit: exact.unit,
99	      packSize: exact.pack_size,
100	      hsnCode: exact.hsn_code,
101	      category: exact.category,
102	      score: 1.0,
103	      method: "exact",
104	    });
105	  }
106	
107	  // Alias cache (HSN-aware) — peek without learning
108	  const alias = await findProductAlias(siteId, name, hsn);
109	  if (alias) {
110	    const p = await import("./db").then((m) =>
111	      m.findCanonicalProduct(alias.product_id),
112	    );
113	    if (p) {
114	      add({
115	        productId: p.product_id,
116	        canonicalName: p.canonical_name,
117	        unit: p.unit,
118	        packSize: p.pack_size,
119	        hsnCode: p.hsn_code,
120	        category: p.category,
121	        score: alias.confidence,
122	        method: "alias",
123	      });
124	    }
125	  }
126	
127	  // HSN-scoped fuzzy: top candidates in same HSN bucket
128	  if (hsn) {
129	    const candidates = await listCanonicalProductsByHsn(siteId, hsn);
130	    for (const c of candidates) {
131	      const score = fuzzyScore(name, c.canonical_name);
132	      if (score >= 0.4) {
133	        add({
134	          productId: c.product_id,
135	          canonicalName: c.canonical_name,
136	          unit: c.unit,
137	          packSize: c.pack_size,
138	          hsnCode: c.hsn_code,
139	          category: c.category,
140	          score,
141	          method: "hsn_fuzzy",
142	        });
143	      }
144	    }
145	  }
146	
147	  // LLM normalization — if it maps to an existing canonical, surface it
148	  try {
149	    const normalized = await normalizeProductName({ siteId, rawName: name });
150	    if (normalized.confidence >= 0.6) {
151	      const product = await findCanonicalProductByName(siteId, normalized.canonical);
152	      if (product) {
153	        add({
154	          productId: product.product_id,
155	          canonicalName: product.canonical_name,
156	          unit: product.unit,
157	          packSize: product.pack_size,
158	          hsnCode: product.hsn_code,
159	          category: product.category,
160	          score: normalized.confidence,
161	          method: "normalized",
162	        });
163	      }
164	    }
165	  } catch {
166	    // ignore LLM errors — fall through
167	  }
168	
169	  // Last-resort: global fuzzy across the whole catalog (no HSN). Cheap on
170	  // small catalogs; if it ever bites, switch to a fuse-style index.
171	  if (pool.size === 0) {
172	    const all = await import("./db").then((m) => m.listCanonicalProducts(siteId));
173	    for (const c of all) {
174	      const score = fuzzyScore(name, c.canonical_name);
175	      if (score >= 0.4) {
176	        add({
177	          productId: c.product_id,
178	          canonicalName: c.canonical_name,
179	          unit: c.unit,
180	          packSize: c.pack_size,
181	          hsnCode: c.hsn_code,
182	          category: c.category,
183	          score,
184	          method: "global_fuzzy",
185	        });
186	      }
187	    }
188	  }
189	
190	  return Array.from(pool.values())
191	    .sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score)
192	    .slice(0, topN);
193	}
194	
195	/**
196	 * Match a single bill line item to a canonical product.
197	 */
198	export async function matchBillLineToProduct(
199	  siteId: string,
200	  item: BillLineItem,
201	): Promise<MatchResult> {
202	  const name = item.item_purchased.trim();
203	  const hsn = item.hsn_code?.trim() || null;
204	
205	  // Step 1: Exact name match in canonical_products
206	  const exact = await findCanonicalProductByName(siteId, name);
207	  if (exact) {
208	    return {
209	      matched: true,
210	      productId: exact.product_id,
211	      canonicalName: exact.canonical_name,
212	      confidence: 1.0,
213	      method: "exact",
214	    };
215	  }
216	
217	  // Step 2: Product alias cache hit (HSN-aware)
218	  const alias = await findProductAlias(siteId, name, hsn);
219	  if (alias && alias.confidence >= 0.7) {
220	    return {
221	      matched: true,
222	      productId: alias.product_id,
223	      canonicalName: alias.raw_name,
224	      confidence: alias.confidence,
225	      method: "alias",
226	    };
227	  }
228	
229	  // Step 3: HSN-scoped fuzzy match
230	  if (hsn) {
231	    const candidates = await listCanonicalProductsByHsn(siteId, hsn);
232	    if (candidates.length > 0) {
233	      let bestMatch: CanonicalProduct | null = null;
234	      let bestScore = 0;
235	
236	      for (const candidate of candidates) {
237	        const score = fuzzyScore(name, candidate.canonical_name);
238	        if (score > bestScore) {
239	          bestScore = score;
240	          bestMatch = candidate;
241	        }
242	      }
243	
244	      if (bestMatch && bestScore >= 0.6) {
245	        // Learn this alias for next time
246	        await saveProductAlias({
247	          id: `pa_${nanoid(12)}`,
248	          siteId,
249	          rawName: name,
250	          productId: bestMatch.product_id,
251	          hsnCode: hsn,
252	          confidence: bestScore,
253	          source: "hsn_fuzzy",
254	        });
255	
256	        return {
257	          matched: true,
258	          productId: bestMatch.product_id,
259	          canonicalName: bestMatch.canonical_name,
260	          confidence: bestScore,
261	          method: "hsn_fuzzy",
262	        };
263	      }
264	    }
265	  }
266	
267	  // Step 4: LLM normalization
268	  try {
269	    const normalized = await normalizeProductName({ siteId, rawName: name });
270	    if (normalized.confidence >= 0.7) {
271	      const product = await findCanonicalProductByName(
272	        siteId,
273	        normalized.canonical,
274	      );
275	      if (product) {
276	        // Learn alias
277	        await saveProductAlias({
278	          id: `pa_${nanoid(12)}`,
279	          siteId,
280	          rawName: name,
281	          productId: product.product_id,
282	          hsnCode: hsn,
283	          confidence: normalized.confidence,
284	          source: "llm_normalized",
285	        });
286	
287	        return {
288	          matched: true,
289	          productId: product.product_id,
290	          canonicalName: product.canonical_name,
291	          confidence: normalized.confidence,
292	          method: "normalized",
293	        };
294	      }
295	    }
296	  } catch {
297	    // LLM failed — fall through to unresolved
298	  }
299	
300	  // Step 5: Unresolved
301	  return {
302	    matched: false,
303	    productId: null,
304	    canonicalName: name,
305	    confidence: 0,
306	    method: "unresolved",
307	  };
308	}
309	
310	export type ValidatedBillItem = {
311	  /** "use_existing" maps the bill line to a known canonical_product.
312	   *  "create_new" registers a new canonical_product. */
313	  action: "use_existing" | "create_new";
314	  product_id?: string | null; // required when action = "use_existing"
315	  canonical_name: string;
316	  category?: string | null;
317	  hsn_code?: string | null;
318	  unit: string;
319	  pack_size: number;
320	  qty: number;
321	  rate?: number | null;
322	  value?: number | null;
323	  bill_item_name: string; // verbatim from the bill — saved as alias
324	};
325	
326	export type ValidatedBill = {
327	  vendor_name?: string | null;
328	  bill_number?: string | null;
329	  bill_date?: string | null;
330	  bill_photo_url?: string | null;
331	  grand_total?: number | null;
332	  items: ValidatedBillItem[];
333	};
334	
335	/**
336	 * Commit an admin-validated bill: create new canonical products as
337	 * needed, write the order + order_items, save aliases. Returns the
338	 * created orderId + per-item commit info so the caller can append
339	 * to the "Ordered" Google Sheet tab.
340	 */
341	export async function processValidatedBill(
342	  siteId: string,
343	  createdBy: string,
344	  bill: ValidatedBill,
345	): Promise<{
346	  orderId: string;
347	  committed: Array<{
348	    productId: string;
349	    canonicalName: string;
350	    unit: string;
351	    packSize: number;
352	    billItemName: string;
353	    hsnCode: string | null;
354	    qty: number;
355	    rate: number | null;
356	    value: number | null;
357	  }>;
358	}> {
359	  const orderId = `ord_${nanoid(12)}`;
360	  await createOrderFromBill({
361	    id: orderId,
362	    siteId,
363	    vendor: bill.vendor_name ?? null,
364	    billNumber: bill.bill_number ?? null,
365	    billDate: bill.bill_date ?? null,
366	    billPhotoUrl: bill.bill_photo_url ?? null,
367	    grandTotal: bill.grand_total ?? null,
368	    expectedAt: bill.bill_date ?? new Date().toISOString(),
369	  });
370	
371	  const committed: Array<{
372	    productId: string;
373	    canonicalName: string;
374	    unit: string;
375	    packSize: number;
376	    billItemName: string;
377	    hsnCode: string | null;
378	    qty: number;
379	    rate: number | null;
380	    value: number | null;
381	  }> = [];
382	
383	  for (const item of bill.items) {
384	    let productId = item.product_id ?? null;
385	    let canonicalName = item.canonical_name;
386	    let unit = item.unit;
387	    let packSize = item.pack_size;
388	
389	    if (item.action === "create_new" || !productId) {
390	      // Admin may have typed a name that already exists in the catalog
391	      // (the matcher missed it, or they didn't realize). Reuse rather
392	      // than insert — the (site_id, canonical_name) unique constraint
393	      // would reject a duplicate insert anyway.
394	      const existing = await findCanonicalProductByName(siteId, canonicalName);
395	      if (existing) {
396	        productId = existing.product_id;
397	        unit = existing.unit;
398	        packSize = existing.pack_size;
399	        canonicalName = existing.canonical_name;
400	      } else {
401	        productId = `prod_${nanoid(10)}`;
402	        await upsertCanonicalProduct({
403	          productId,
404	          siteId,
405	          canonicalName,
406	          category: item.category ?? null,
407	          hsnCode: item.hsn_code ?? null,
408	          unit,
409	          packSize,
410	          createdBy,
411	        });
412	      }
413	    } else {
414	      // use_existing — keep server canonical_name / unit / pack_size canonical
415	      // but record what the admin saw. If admin edited canonical_name, the
416	      // existing canonical product takes precedence (don't silently rename
417	      // catalog rows under the admin).
418	      const existing = await findCanonicalProductByName(siteId, canonicalName);
419	      if (existing) {
420	        productId = existing.product_id;
421	        unit = existing.unit;
422	        packSize = existing.pack_size;
423	        canonicalName = existing.canonical_name;
424	      }
425	    }
426	
427	    // Save the alias so this vendor's variant of the name auto-resolves next time
428	    if (item.bill_item_name && item.bill_item_name.trim() !== canonicalName) {
429	      try {
430	        await saveProductAlias({
431	          id: `pa_${nanoid(12)}`,
432	          siteId,
433	          rawName: item.bill_item_name,
434	          productId,
435	          hsnCode: item.hsn_code ?? null,
436	          confidence: 1.0,
437	          source: "admin_validated",
438	        });
439	      } catch {
440	        // unique-violation on rawname+hsn is fine — already learned
441	      }
442	    }
443	
444	    const orderItemId = `oi_${nanoid(12)}`;
445	    await createOrderItemFromBill({
446	      id: orderItemId,
447	      orderId,
448	      productId,
449	      productName: canonicalName,
450	      billItemName: item.bill_item_name,
451	      hsnCode: item.hsn_code ?? null,
452	      expectedQty: item.qty,
453	      unitRate: item.rate ?? null,
454	      lineValue: item.value ?? null,
455	      matchMethod: item.action === "create_new" ? "admin_new" : "admin_validated",
456	      matchConfidence: 1.0,
457	    });
458	
459	    committed.push({
460	      productId,
461	      canonicalName,
462	      unit,
463	      packSize,
464	      billItemName: item.bill_item_name,
465	      hsnCode: item.hsn_code ?? null,
466	      qty: item.qty,
467	      rate: item.rate ?? null,
468	      value: item.value ?? null,
469	    });
470	  }
471	
472	  return { orderId, committed };
473	}
474	
475	/**
476	 * Process an entire extracted bill: match each line item, create
477	 * an order, and queue unresolved items for admin resolution.
478	 *
479	 * Returns the created order ID and per-line-item match results.
480	 */
481	export async function processBill(
482	  siteId: string,
483	  bill: ExtractedBill,
484	  billPhotoUrl?: string | null,
485	): Promise<{
486	  orderId: string;
487	  results: Array<{
488	    lineItem: BillLineItem;
489	    match: MatchResult;
490	    orderItemId: string | null;
491	  }>;
492	  summary: {
493	    total: number;
494	    matched: number;
495	    unresolved: number;
496	  };
497	}> {
498	  const orderId = `ord_${nanoid(12)}`;
499	
500	  // Create the order
501	  await createOrderFromBill({
502	    id: orderId,
503	    siteId,
504	    vendor: bill.vendor_name,
505	    billNumber: bill.bill_number,
506	    billDate: bill.bill_date,
507	    billPhotoUrl: billPhotoUrl ?? null,
508	    grandTotal: bill.grand_total,
509	    expectedAt: bill.bill_date ?? new Date().toISOString(),
510	  });
511	
512	  const results: Array<{
513	    lineItem: BillLineItem;
514	    match: MatchResult;
515	    orderItemId: string | null;
516	  }> = [];
517	
518	  let matchedCount = 0;
519	  let unresolvedCount = 0;
520	
521	  for (const lineItem of bill.line_items) {
522	    const match = await matchBillLineToProduct(siteId, lineItem);
523	
524	    if (match.matched && match.productId) {
525	      // Create order item linked to canonical product
526	      const orderItemId = `oi_${nanoid(12)}`;
527	      await createOrderItemFromBill({
528	        id: orderItemId,
529	        orderId,
530	        productId: match.productId,
531	        productName: match.canonicalName,
532	        billItemName: lineItem.item_purchased,
533	        hsnCode: lineItem.hsn_code,
534	        expectedQty: lineItem.qty,
535	        unitRate: lineItem.rate,
536	        lineValue: lineItem.value,
537	        matchMethod: match.method,
538	        matchConfidence: match.confidence,
539	      });
540	
541	      results.push({ lineItem, match, orderItemId });
542	      matchedCount++;
543	    } else {
544	      // Queue for admin resolution
545	      const unresolvedId = `ubli_${nanoid(12)}`;
546	      await insertUnresolvedBillItem({
547	        id: unresolvedId,
548	        siteId,
549	        orderId,
550	        billItemName: lineItem.item_purchased,
551	        hsnCode: lineItem.hsn_code,
552	        qty: lineItem.qty,
553	        unitRate: lineItem.rate,
554	        lineValue: lineItem.value,
555	      });
556	
557	      results.push({ lineItem, match, orderItemId: null });
558	      unresolvedCount++;
559	    }
560	  }
561	
562	  return {
563	    orderId,
564	    results,
565	    summary: {
566	      total: bill.line_items.length,
567	      matched: matchedCount,
568	      unresolved: unresolvedCount,
569	    },
570	  };
571	}

> AGENT

Found it. The alias is being saved correctly on the backend — but the **dashboard refuses to auto-apply it on the next upload**. So the system "knows" the answer; it just makes you click it again every time.

## The full chain

**On the first upload (when you renamed "TATA SALT 1KG IODIZED" → "Tata Salt"):**

1. Dashboard sends `validateBill` with `canonical_name: "Tata Salt"`, `bill_item_name: "TATA SALT 1KG IODIZED"`.
2. `processValidatedBill` runs the alias save at `bill-matcher.ts:428-442`:
   ```ts
   if (item.bill_item_name && item.bill_item_name.trim() !== canonicalName) {
     await saveProductAlias({
       rawName: item.bill_item_name,
       productId,
       hsnCode: item.hsn_code,
       confidence: 1.0,
       source: "admin_validated",
     });
   }
   ```
3. ✅ Row in `product_aliases`: `raw_name="TATA SALT 1KG IODIZED"`, `product_id=<Tata Salt>`, `source="admin_validated"`, `confidence=1.0`.

**On the second upload of the same bill:**

4. `suggestForBillLine` runs (`bill-matcher.ts:79`). The alias path at line 108 hits — it returns a suggestion with `method: "alias"`, `score: 1.0`, `canonicalName: "Tata Salt"`. ✅ Backend is correct.
5. Dashboard receives the suggestion list and runs `itemToRow` at `InventorySection.tsx:640`. The auto-apply check is:
   ```ts
   const exact = it.suggestions.find(
     (s) =>
       s.method === "exact" ||
       s.canonicalName.trim().toLowerCase() === it.bill_item_name.trim().toLowerCase(),
   );
   ```
6. The alias hit doesn't match: `method` is `"alias"` (not `"exact"`), and the canonical name "Tata Salt" doesn't equal the bill name "TATA SALT 1KG IODIZED". So the row falls through to `action: "create_new"`, canonical_name pre-filled with the raw bill text. **The alias is shown only as a suggestion chip you can click.**

## Why it was written that way

The comment right above that block (`InventorySection.tsx:644-647`) calls out the reasoning: a previous "alias cache poisoning" incident — wrong aliases auto-applying the wrong answer forever. So the previous author whitelisted only `method="exact"` for auto-apply.

That's overcautious. Aliases written by `admin_validated` come from an explicit admin choice with `confidence: 1.0`. By definition they're the strongest mapping signal you can have. Treating them the same as "exact-name match" is correct; only `hsn_fuzzy` / `normalized` / `global_fuzzy` need the manual-confirm gate.

## A second risk that could also bite you

OCR isn't byte-identical between runs of the same bill. If the first scan produced `"TATA SALT 1KG IODIZED"` and the second scan produces `"TATA SALT 1Kg IODISED"` (different spelling / casing / spacing), even an admin-validated alias would miss because the lookup at `db.ts:findProductAlias` is `lower(raw_name) = lower(?)` — exact string match after lowercase. So the alias is on file but never gets queried. You'd need to scan the same bill text-perfectly twice for it to hit.

## What I'd fix

1. **Auto-apply admin-validated aliases.** Change the `itemToRow` filter to also accept `method === "alias"` when the suggestion's score is 1.0 (which only `admin_validated` reaches). One condition added.

2. **Normalise alias `raw_name` more aggressively** so OCR jitter doesn't defeat the cache. Strip punctuation, collapse whitespace, optionally drop trailing pack-size text. Store the normalised form alongside the original. Match against the normalised form. This is a backend change in `saveProductAlias` and `findProductAlias` plus a one-shot migration to normalise existing rows.

3. **Add alias-fuzzy fallback.** Currently `hsn_fuzzy` only compares against canonical names. The learned aliases ARE the proven "names vendors use" labels — they should be included as fuzzy candidates too, so even a slightly-different OCR string maps to the right product. Backend change in `matchBillLineToProduct` / `suggestForBillLine`.

(1) alone fixes the reported behavior for the case where OCR is consistent. (2) and (3) close the OCR-jitter gap. They're independent — we can ship (1) now and decide on (2)/(3) after seeing how often jitter actually bites.

Want me to:
- Ship (1) only? (smallest, fixes the user-visible bug now)
- Ship (1) + (3)? (skip the migration; gets fuzzy-on-aliases working)
- Ship all three?

  "old_string": "export async function listProductAliases(\n  productId: string,\n): Promise<ProductAlias[]> {\n  const db = await getDb();\n  const result = await db.execute({\n    sql: `SELECT * FROM product_aliases WHERE product_id = ? ORDER BY raw_name`,\n    args: [productId],\n  });\n  return result.rows as ProductAlias[];\n}",
  "new_string": "export async function listProductAliases(\n  productId: string,\n): Promise<ProductAlias[]> {\n  const db = await getDb();\n  const result = await db.execute({\n    sql: `SELECT * FROM product_aliases WHERE product_id = ? ORDER BY raw_name`,\n    args: [productId],\n  });\n  return result.rows as ProductAlias[];\n}\n\n/**\n * Every alias on this site, optionally restricted to a single HSN\n * bucket. Used by the bill matcher's fuzzy step so that an OCR'd line\n * like \"TATA SALT 1Kg IODISED\" can hit an admin-validated alias\n * \"TATA SALT 1KG IODIZED\" even though the two strings aren't byte-\n * identical (different casing, missing letter, etc.). The HSN-scoped\n * call is preferred — it scales with bucket size rather than the whole\n * catalog. The unscoped variant is the global-fallback fuzzy.\n *\n * Catalog and alias counts at any single site are small (low thousands\n * at most), so we don't bother with an index or pagination — Postgres\n * will sequential-scan with a HASH JOIN at this scale and still be\n * sub-millisecond.\n */\nexport async function listProductAliasesForSite(\n  siteId: string,\n  hsnCode?: string | null,\n): Promise<ProductAlias[]> {\n  const db = await getDb();\n  if (hsnCode) {\n    const result = await db.execute({\n      sql: `SELECT * FROM product_aliases WHERE site_id = ? AND hsn_code = ?`,\n      args: [siteId, hsnCode],\n    });\n    return result.rows as ProductAlias[];\n  }\n  const result = await db.execute({\n    sql: `SELECT * FROM product_aliases WHERE site_id = ?`,\n    args: [siteId],\n  });\n  return result.rows as ProductAlias[];\n}"
}

Aliases ARE the strongest \"names vendors\n * use\" signal, but an OCR-jitter near-miss on an alias is one step\n * removed from an admin's deliberate choice — we want the auto-apply\n * threshold to require a high underlying similarity, not just \"above\n * 0.6\". {
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/appointy/work/OfficeOperationsUmbrella/office-ops-engine/src/mastra/modules/inventory/bill-matcher.ts",
  "old_string": "export type Suggestion = {\n  productId: string;\n  canonicalName: string;\n  unit: string;\n  packSize: number;\n  hsnCode: string | null;\n  category: string | null;\n  score: number;\n  method: \"exact\" | \"alias\" | \"hsn_fuzzy\" | \"normalized\" | \"global_fuzzy\";\n};",
  "new_string": "export type Suggestion = {\n  productId: string;\n  canonicalName: string;\n  unit: string;\n  packSize: number;\n  hsnCode: string | null;\n  category: string | null;\n  score: number;\n  method: \"exact\" | \"alias\" | \"alias_fuzzy\" | \"hsn_fuzzy\" | \"normalized\" | \"global_fuzzy\";\n};\n\n/**\n * Run alias-fuzzy: compare the bill line name against every learned\n * alias' raw_name and surface the highest-scoring product. HSN-scoped\n * when available so an alias for \"Toor Dal\" under HSN 0713 doesn't\n * compete with \"Toor Dal Snack Pack\" under a different HSN.\n *\n * Returns at most one candidate per product_id (multiple aliases for\n * the same product are normal — we keep the best-scoring one). Below\n * threshold = no candidate, the caller falls through to the next step.\n */\nasync function bestAliasFuzzyMatch(\n  siteId: string,\n  rawName: string,\n  hsn: string | null,\n  threshold: number,\n): Promise<{ alias: ProductAlias; product: CanonicalProduct; score: number } | null> {\n  const aliases = await listProductAliasesForSite(siteId, hsn);\n  if (aliases.length === 0) return null;\n\n  // Group by product_id, keep best-scoring alias per product.\n  type Best = { alias: ProductAlias; score: number };\n  const bestByProduct = new Map<string, Best>();\n  for (const a of aliases) {\n    const score = fuzzyScore(rawName, a.raw_name);\n    if (score < threshold) continue;\n    const cur = bestByProduct.get(a.product_id);\n    if (!cur || score > cur.score) bestByProduct.set(a.product_id, { alias: a, score });\n  }\n  if (bestByProduct.size === 0) return null;\n\n  // Pick the overall winner.\n  let winner: { productId: string; alias: ProductAlias; score: number } | null = null;\n  for (const [productId, b] of bestByProduct) {\n    if (!winner || b.score > winner.score) winner = { productId, alias: b.alias, score: b.score };\n  }\n  if (!winner) return null;\n\n  const product = await findCanonicalProduct(winner.productId);\n  if (!product) return null;\n  return { alias: winner.alias, product, score: winner.score };\n}"
}

  "new_string": "  // HSN-scoped fuzzy: top candidates in same HSN bucket\n  if (hsn) {\n    const candidates = await listCanonicalProductsByHsn(siteId, hsn);\n    for (const c of candidates) {\n      const score = fuzzyScore(name, c.canonical_name);\n      if (score >= 0.4) {\n        add({\n          productId: c.product_id,\n          canonicalName: c.canonical_name,\n          unit: c.unit,\n          packSize: c.pack_size,\n          hsnCode: c.hsn_code,\n          category: c.category,\n          score,\n          method: \"hsn_fuzzy\",\n        });\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Alias-fuzzy: an OCR'd name often differs slightly from the alias\n  // an admin previously validated (\"TATA SALT 1KG IODISED\" vs\n  // \"TATA SALT 1KG IODIZED\"). Comparing against learned aliases catches\n  // those near-misses that pure canonical-name fuzzy can't (the\n  // canonical name is the admin's preferred short form like \"Tata\n  // Salt\", which shares almost no bigrams with the raw bill text).\n  //\n  // HSN-scoped first, then a global pass if HSN-scoped returned nothing.\n  // The 0.6 threshold matches the per-step gates used elsewhere; we\n  // apply the discount in `add()` so a borderline alias-fuzzy hit\n  // doesn't outrank a stronger alias / exact hit if one also exists.\n  let aliasFuzzy = await bestAliasFuzzyMatch(siteId, name, hsn, 0.6);\n  if (!aliasFuzzy && hsn) {\n    // Same alias text might be stored under a different (or null) HSN\n    // for the same product — try once more without HSN scope.\n    aliasFuzzy = await bestAliasFuzzyMatch(siteId, name, null, 0.6);\n  }\n  if (!aliasFuzzy && !hsn) {\n    // No HSN on the current bill — the first call already covered the\n    // global pool, nothing more to do.\n  }\n  if (aliasFuzzy) {\n    add({\n      productId: aliasFuzzy.product.product_id,\n      canonicalName: aliasFuzzy.product.canonical_name,\n      unit: aliasFuzzy.product.unit,\n      packSize: aliasFuzzy.product.pack_size,\n      hsnCode: aliasFuzzy.product.hsn_code,\n      category: aliasFuzzy.product.category,\n      score: aliasFuzzy.score * ALIAS_FUZZY_SCORE_DISCOUNT,\n      method: \"alias_fuzzy\",\n    });\n  }\n\n  // LLM normalization — if it maps to an existing canonical, surface it"

> AGENT

Both subrepos clean (visitor errors are pre-existing, unrelated).

## Summary of changes

### (1) Dashboard auto-applies admin-validated aliases

`ops-dashboard/src/modules/inventory/InventorySection.tsx` — `itemToRow` filter now also accepts `method === "alias" && score >= 1`. Aliases written by `admin_validated` always carry `confidence: 1.0`, so they auto-apply the next time the same bill text comes through. Fuzzy / normalized / global-fuzzy hits stay as clickable suggestions (preserves the original safety net against cache poisoning).

### (3) Backend alias-fuzzy fallback

`office-ops-engine/src/mastra/modules/inventory/db.ts` — new `listProductAliasesForSite(siteId, hsnCode?)` that returns every alias on a site (HSN-scoped when provided, full set when null).

`office-ops-engine/src/mastra/modules/inventory/bill-matcher.ts`:
- New `alias_fuzzy` method (added to `MatchResult.method` and `Suggestion.method`).
- New `bestAliasFuzzyMatch()` helper: compares the OCR'd name against every learned alias' `raw_name`, groups by `product_id` (keep best score per product), returns the highest-scoring product overall.
- `ALIAS_FUZZY_SCORE_DISCOUNT = 0.95` applied to fuzzy-alias scores so a borderline alias-fuzzy hit can't outrank a stronger exact / direct alias suggestion if one also exists.
- **`suggestForBillLine`** runs alias-fuzzy at 0.6 threshold, HSN-scoped first then global fallback. The suggestion goes into the UI's chip list with `method: "alias_fuzzy"`.
- **`matchBillLineToProduct`** runs alias-fuzzy at 0.75 threshold (higher than the suggest path because this writes an order line silently — we want OCR-jitter near-hits, not loose fuzzy guesses). On a hit, it **also saves the new exact name as a fresh alias** so the next pass takes the cheap Step-2 exact-alias path instead of recomputing fuzzy across the whole alias set.

### What's behavior-shifted

- First scan of "TATA SALT 1KG IODIZED" → admin renames to "Tata Salt" → alias saved with `source="admin_validated"`, `confidence=1.0`.
- Second scan of the same text → exact alias hit → dashboard auto-applies (uses `use_existing`, pre-fills canonical + unit + pack + HSN). No clicking required.
- Second scan with OCR jitter ("IODISED" / different casing / extra whitespace) → exact alias misses → alias-fuzzy hits at score ~0.85, surfaces a top suggestion with method `alias_fuzzy`. **It is NOT auto-applied** (score < 1) — admin still confirms with a click, but at least the right suggestion is up top instead of buried.
- After that confirm-click, the new exact alias gets saved → the third scan with the same OCR jitter auto-applies.

### What does NOT change

- The 0.75 silent-match threshold in `matchBillLineToProduct` is the same gate that existed for HSN-fuzzy — alias-fuzzy uses the same bar, no new silent-commit risk.
- HSN-fuzzy on canonical names is untouched. Both paths now run; suggestions get pooled and ranked.
- `processValidatedBill` and `findProductAlias` are unchanged. The exact-alias write path that powers (1) was already correct on the backend.

Nothing committed.

> AGENT

Here's the plan for Phase 2, straight from the doc plus the practical sequencing.

## Phase 2 — what gets built

Two independent workstreams that can ship in parallel:

### A. Incremental catalog sync (small, low-risk)

- **Backend**: extend `GET /api/inventory/canonical-products` with an optional `?since=<ISO>` query param. When present, returns only rows where `updated_at > since`. Without it, returns full snapshot (today's behavior — preserves existing callers).
- **Field-app**: `syncCanonicalProducts()` tracks the max `updated_at` from the last sync in AsyncStorage. Next call sends it as `?since=...`. Most syncs become near-empty payloads instead of the full catalog.
- **Deletes**: incremental sync misses them. Solution: **run a full-replace once an hour** to reconcile. Cheap, no schema work, no tombstones. Inside the field-app sync timer: track "last full sync at"; if >60 min, drop the `since` param for that one call.

### B. SSE for real-time freshness (the big one)

Done in this strict order — each step gates the next:

1. **Bifrost ingress verification (go/no-go).** Stand up a temporary trivial SSE endpoint on `ops-engine` — just sends a heartbeat every 10 s. Deploy. Curl it through the public URL. Verify the connection stays open >2 minutes, events flow, no buffering. **If this fails, SSE is dead** — we'd need to talk to Bifrost ops about `X-Accel-Buffering`, `proxy_read_timeout`, etc., before anything else gets built. This is the cheapest way to find out.

2. **Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY` backbone.** New `office-ops-engine/src/mastra/modules/inventory/events.ts`. One persistent connection per `ops-engine` process running `LISTEN inventory_changes`. Exposes `emitChange(siteId, topic, payload)` which fires `NOTIFY inventory_changes, '<json>'`. In-process subscribers (the SSE endpoint) get the notification delivered on the listening connection's callback.

3. **Per-connection ring buffer.** Each connected SSE client gets an in-memory deque of recent events (~last 100). When a client reconnects with `Last-Event-ID: <n>`, we replay anything missed in the gap. Site-scoped — guards never see other sites' events.

4. **SSE endpoint.** `GET /api/inventory/events?token=<...>&siteId=<...>`. Token validation reuses `validateGuardSession` from the guard auth path. Heartbeat write every 15 s to keep proxies from closing. Per-topic debouncing (500 ms coalesce window) so a delivery scan that fires 50 stock notifications becomes ~one event per topic.

5. **Write-path integration.** Every committed write calls `emitChange(siteId, topic, hint)`:
   - `receipt` → topic `stock` + `orders`
   - `issue` / `dispense` / `stock_correction` → topic `stock`
   - `learn_barcode`, `canonical-products` POST/PATCH → topic `catalog`
   - `mismatch_flag` → topic `orders`
   Payload is hint-only: `{ barcode?: "...", orderId?: "..." }`. The client refetches the full row, not what's in the event.

6. **Mobile SSE client.** New `field-app/src/sync/events.ts`. Use `react-native-sse` (actively maintained). Manages:
   - Connection lifecycle — open on session present, close on logout / 401.
   - **Jittered backoff** on reconnect: random delay 0-5 s. Without this, 100 clients reconnect simultaneously when `ops-engine` restarts.
   - **Foreground reconnect** — Android suspends sockets when the app backgrounds. Hook `AppState` and reconnect on resume.
   - **`Last-Event-ID` resume** — persist the latest seen id to AsyncStorage so cold-boot also resumes.

7. **Client dispatcher.** Map SSE topic → `invalidateRefetchThrottle(key)` + force-refresh the matching cache. Reuses the existing cache-status machinery from Phase 1 — no new render plumbing, the screens already react to `cache.warm()` transitions.

8. **Keep polling as fallback for 2-4 weeks.** Don't tear down the 30 s `flushOnce` timer until SSE is proven on the real device fleet. If SSE flakes on patchy Wi-Fi or specific Android versions, polling still catches users up.

## What I'd skip until later

- **WebSocket** — bidirectional capability we don't need; sticking with SSE.
- **Redis pub/sub** — Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY` is enough until `ops-engine` scales beyond one process. Future change, not now.
- **Tombstones for deletes in incremental sync** — periodic full-replace is simpler and works.
- **Server-side catalog response caching** — premature; the queries are cheap.
- **JIT removal** — JIT verification stays. SSE makes JIT *almost always* return "no change", but it's still the correctness floor for the milliseconds-race case and for offline catch-up.

## Concrete shippable units (one per PR/session)

1. Incremental catalog sync (~2 hours, fully independent of SSE work).
2. SSE smoke endpoint + Bifrost verification (~1 hour, **decides whether the rest happens**).
3. Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY` plumbing + `emitChange` helper + write-path integration (~1/2 day).
4. SSE endpoint with auth + heartbeat + ring buffer (~1/2 day).
5. Mobile SSE client + dispatcher (~1 day).
6. Soak observation, then teardown of legacy polling timer (~2-4 weeks elapsed).

Three quick decisions before I start:

1. **Sequence:** ship incremental sync (A) first, then start the SSE chain (B)? Or do (B1) Bifrost verification first because it's the riskiest unknown?
2. **Topics granularity:** four topics (`stock`, `orders`, `catalog`, `alerts`) or finer-grained (`stock:<barcode>`)? Coarser = fewer events, more client refetches; finer = inverse. I'd recommend coarse — debounce already handles burst load and the refetches are cheap.
3. **Site scoping:** today everything is `site-dev`. SSE will subscribe by `siteId`. Confirm there's no scenario where one device should see events from multiple sites?

99	function envFlag(value: string | undefined): boolean | undefined {
100	  if (value === undefined) return undefined;
101	  const normalized = value.trim().toLowerCase();
102	  if (["1", "true", "yes", "on"].includes(normalized)) return true;
103	  if (["0", "false", "no", "off"].includes(normalized)) return false;
104	  return undefined;
105	}

109	  if (explicit === false) return false;

111	    throw new Error("TELEGRAM_ENABLED=true requires TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN.");
112	  }
113	  return Boolean(process.env.TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN);
114	}

237	  async sendMessage(chatId: string, text: string, options?: SendMessageOptions): Promise<void> {
238	    const parseMode = options?.parseMode === "markdown" ? "Markdown" as const
239	      : options?.parseMode === "html" ? "HTML" as const
240	      : undefined;

242	      parseMode,
243	      replyToMessageId: options?.replyToMessageId ? Number(options.replyToMessageId) : undefined,
244	    });

248	    const adminIds = tgConfig.adminIds();
249	    const parseMode = options?.parseMode === "markdown" ? "Markdown" as const
250	      : options?.parseMode === "html" ? "HTML" as const
251	      : undefined;
252	
253	    for (const adminId of adminIds) {
254	      try {
255	        await sendMessage(adminId, text, { parseMode });
256	      } catch (error) {
257	        logWarn(`Failed to notify Telegram admin ${adminId}: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`);
258	      }
259	    }
260	  },
261	};

263	// ─── Start Telegram Channel ──────────────────────────────

265	async function startTelegramChannel(): Promise<void> {
266	  const bot = getBot();
267	
268	  // ─── Graceful Shutdown ───────────────────────────────────
269	  let shuttingDown = false;
270	  const shutdown = async () => {
271	    if (shuttingDown) return;
272	    shuttingDown = true;
273	    logInfo("Shutting down bot", { scope: "server" });
274	    await telegramAdapter.shutdown();
275	    setTimeout(() => process.exit(0), 3000).unref();
276	    process.exit(0);
277	  };
278	  process.on("SIGINT", shutdown);
279	  process.on("SIGTERM", shutdown);
280	  process.on("SIGHUP", shutdown);
281	  process.on("beforeExit", shutdown);
282	
283	  // ─── Polling Error Handler ──────────────────────────────
284	  bot.on("polling_error", (error: any) => {
285	    logError("Telegram polling error", error?.message || error, {
286	      scope: "telegram.polling",
287	    });
288	    if (error?.message?.includes("409")) {
289	      logError(
290	        "Another bot instance is running. Stop the duplicate process and restart.",
291	        undefined,
292	        { scope: "telegram.polling" },
293	      );
294	    }
295	  });

297	  // ─── Raw Update Diagnostics ─────────────────────────────
298	  (bot as any).on("update", (update: any) => {
299	    const type =
300	      Object.keys(update)
301	        .filter((k) => k !== "update_id")
302	        .join(", ") || "unknown";
303	    const id = update.update_id;
304	    if (update.callback_query) {
305	      const cb = update.callback_query;
306	      logInfo(
307	        `Telegram update received: callback_query data="${cb.data}" from=${cb.from?.first_name} (${cb.from?.id}) msg_id=${cb.message?.message_id}`,
308	        {
309	          scope: "telegram.update",
310	          eventId: String(id),
311	          chatId: cb.message?.chat?.id,
312	          userId: cb.from?.id,
313	          messageId: cb.message?.message_id,
314	          action: cb.data,
315	        },
316	      );
317	    } else {
318	      logInfo(`Telegram update received: ${type}`, {
319	        scope: "telegram.update",
320	        eventId: String(id),
321	      });
322	    }
323	  });
324	
325	  await registerBotCommands();
326	
327	  // ─── Message Handler ────────────────────────────────────
328	  // Translates Telegram messages to StandardMessage and routes to core.
329	  bot.on("message", async (msg: TelegramBot.Message) => {
330	    try {
331	      const scope = isPrivateMessage(msg)
332	        ? "telegram.dm"
333	        : isGroupMessage(msg)
334	          ? "telegram.group"
335	          : "telegram.message";
336	      await withLogContext(buildMessageLogContext(scope, msg), async () => {
337	        // Skip bot's own messages
338	        if (msg.from?.is_bot) return;
339	
340	        if (isWhereAmICommand(getMessageText(msg))) {
341	          await replyWithChatDetails(msg);
342	          return;
343	        }
344	
345	        if (isGroupMessage(msg)) {
346	          if (isVisitorManagementGroup(msg.chat.id)) {
347	            logInfo("Ignoring inbound message from visitor management broadcast group", {
348	              scope: "telegram.group.visitor-management",
349	              chatId: msg.chat.id,
350	              userId: msg.from?.id,
351	              messageId: msg.message_id,
352	            });
353	            return;
354	          }
355	
356	          const text = getMessageText(msg);
357	          if (isOfflineSyncText(text)) {
358	            try {
359	              const offlineEvent = parseOfflineSyncText(text ?? "");
360	              const result = await processOfflineSyncEvent({
361	                event: offlineEvent,
362	                source: "telegram",
363	                backupText: text ?? undefined,
364	                sourceChannel: "telegram",
365	                sourceChatId: String(msg.chat.id),
366	                sourceMessageId: String(msg.message_id),
367	              });
368	
369	              logInfo(

371	                {
372	                  scope: "telegram.offline-sync",
373	                  chatId: msg.chat.id,
374	                  userId: msg.from?.id,
375	                  messageId: msg.message_id,
376	                },
377	              );
378	            } catch (offlineError) {

381	              );

385	          }

387	          // Ingest immediately to DB — batch processor handles grouping

392

461	async function startServer() {
462	  installConsoleBridge();
463	  logInfo("Starting Office Operations Intelligence", { scope: "server" });
464	  logInfo("Architecture: Central Intelligence + Connectors", {
465	    scope: "server",
466	  });

469	    { scope: "server.dev" },
470	  );
471	
472	  // ─── Register Channels ─────────────────────────────────
473	  // Each channel registers itself with the core registry.
474	  // Adding a new channel is as simple as:
475	  //   1. Create channels/<name>/adapter.ts
476	  //   2. Register it here
477	
478	  const telegramEnabled = shouldStartTelegramChannel();
479	  if (telegramEnabled) {
480	    registerChannel(telegramAdapter);
481	    registerMediaDownloader("telegram", telegramMediaDownloader);
482	
483	    // Register Telegram-specific config with the core message router
484	    registerChannelConfig("telegram", {
485	      onCommand: async (message, command, args) => {
486	        // Retrieve the raw Telegram message from the StandardMessage payload
487	        const rawMsg = message.rawPayload as TelegramBot.Message;
488	        if (rawMsg) {
489	          await handleCommand(rawMsg, `${command} ${args.join(" ")}`.trim());
490	        }
491	      },
492	      onReply: async (chatId, text, options) => {
493	        const parseMode = options?.parseMode === "markdown" ? "Markdown" as const
494	          : options?.parseMode === "html" ? "HTML" as const
495	          : undefined;
496	        await sendMessage(Number(chatId), text, { parseMode });
497	      },
498	      onTyping: async (chatId) => {
499	        const bot = getBot();
500	        await bot.sendChatAction(Number(chatId), "typing");
501	      },
502	    });
503	
504	    // ─── Start Telegram ────────────────────────────────────
505	    await startTelegramChannel();
506	  } else {
507	    logWarn("Telegram channel disabled. Set TELEGRAM_ENABLED=true and TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN to enable it.", {
508	      scope: "server",
509	    });
510	  }
511	
512	  // ─── Register WhatsApp Channel ───────────────────────────
513	  // Drop-in: the adapter declares its own webhook routes, so we
514	  // only register it. No route mounting needed here.
515	  if (isWhatsAppConfigured()) {
516	    registerChannel(whatsappAdapter);
517	    await whatsappAdapter.initialize();
518	    logInfo("WhatsApp channel registered (webhook at POST/GET /webhook/whatsapp)", {
519	      scope: "server",
520	    });
521	  } else {
522	    logWarn(
523	      "WhatsApp channel disabled. Set WHATSAPP_ACCESS_TOKEN, WHATSAPP_PHONE_NUMBER_ID, and WHATSAPP_VERIFY_TOKEN to enable it.",
524	      { scope: "server" },
525	    );
526	  }
527	
528	  // ─── Initialize System Plugins ───────────────────────────
529	  await initPlugins();
530	
531	  // ─── Seed System Flow Graphs ────────────────────────────
532	  try {
533	    await ensureSeedGraphs();
534	    logInfo("System flow graphs seeded", { scope: "server" });
535	  } catch (err) {
536	    logWarn(`Failed to seed flow graphs: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`, { scope: "server" });
537	  }
538	
539	  // ─── Start Visitor HTTP Server ────────────────────────────
540	  const visitorPort = Number(process.env.VISITOR_HTTP_PORT ?? 4112);
541	  const httpServer = createHttpServer(async (req, res) => {
542	    const method = req.method ?? "GET";
543	    const path = getRequestPathname(req);
544	    const query = getRequestQuery(req);
545	    const ip = getClientIp(req);
546	    const reqId = newRequestId();
547	    const startedAt = Date.now();
548	    const shouldLog = shouldLogHttp(method, path);
549	
550	    if (shouldLog) {
551	      logHttpRequest({ method, path, query: query || undefined, ip, reqId });
552	    }
553	
554	    let responseLogged = false;
555	    const logResponseOnce = () => {
556	      if (responseLogged || !shouldLog) return;
557	      responseLogged = true;
558	      logHttpResponse({
559	        method,
560	        path,
561	        status: res.statusCode,
562	        durationMs: Date.now() - startedAt,
563	        reqId,
564	      });
565	    };
566	    res.on("finish", logResponseOnce);
567	    res.on("close", logResponseOnce);
568	
569	    await withLogContext({ scope: "http", reqId }, async () => {
570	      if (method === "OPTIONS") {
571	        res.writeHead(204, {
572	          ...VISITOR_HTTP_CORS_HEADERS,
573	        });
574	        res.end();
575	        return;
576	      }
577	      if (await handleHealthRequest(req, res)) {
578	        return;
579	      }
580	      try {
581	        // Channel-declared routes (webhooks) take priority — each
582	        // ChannelAdapter publishes its own routes() so the server
583	        // mounts them automatically. Add a channel; it just works.
584	        if (await handleChannelHttpRequest(req, res)) {
585	          return;
586	        }
587	        const handled = await handlePluginHttpRequest(req, res);
588	        if (!handled) {
589	          res.writeHead(404, {
590	            ...VISITOR_HTTP_CORS_HEADERS,
591	            'Content-Type': 'text/plain',
592	          });
593	          res.end('Not found');
594	        }
595	      } catch (error) {
596	        logError('Visitor HTTP handler error', error, { scope: 'visitor.http', reqId });
597	        if (!res.headersSent) {
598	          res.writeHead(500, {
599	            ...VISITOR_HTTP_CORS_HEADERS,
600	            'Content-Type': 'application/json',
601	          });
602	          res.end(JSON.stringify({ error: 'Internal server error' }));
603	        }
604	      }
605	    });
606	  });
607	
608	  httpServer.listen(visitorPort, () => {
609	    logInfo(`Visitor HTTP server on :${visitorPort}`, { scope: 'visitor.http' });
610	    logInfo(`Visitor and ops dashboard frontends should call API origin http://localhost:${visitorPort}`, { scope: 'visitor.http' });
611	  });
612	
613	  // ─── Start Scheduler (platform-agnostic) ───────────────
614	  await setupScheduledTasks();
615	
616	  logInfo("Server is running — all channels active", { scope: "server" });
617	}
618	
619	// ─── Start ────────────────────────────────────────────────
620	
621	startServer().catch((error) => {
622	  logError("Failed to start server", error, { scope: "server" });
623	  process.exit(1);
624	});
625

  "new_string": "  // ── SSE smoke test (Phase 2 — Bifrost ingress verification) ─\n  //\n  // GO/NO-GO probe for the SSE work. Does NOT touch Postgres LISTEN/\n  // NOTIFY, does NOT require auth, does NOT carry real events. It only\n  // proves the stream survives the proxy chain (Bifrost ingress → bun\n  // server → curl/browser) with:\n  //   - text/event-stream content-type\n  //   - X-Accel-Buffering: no (so nginx-family proxies don't buffer)\n  //   - small heartbeat every 10 s so dead-stream detectors stay quiet\n  //   - long-lived response that the server closes after 5 min so a\n  //     stray client doesn't pin a worker forever\n  //\n  // Verification recipe (run from a shell with internet):\n  //\n  //   curl -N -H 'Accept: text/event-stream' \\\n  //     https://ops-engine-dev-330299.bifrost.saastack.site/api/inventory/events/smoke\n  //\n  // Expected: one \"open\" event immediately, then \"tick\" every 10 s,\n  // streaming line-by-line (NOT buffered into a single chunk at the\n  // end). If the connection dies in under ~60 s or events arrive in a\n  // burst at close, the proxy is buffering and Phase 2 SSE work is\n  // blocked until the ingress config is fixed.\n  //\n  // Once Phase 2 SSE proper ships, this endpoint stays as the always-\n  // available \"is SSE working through the proxy\" smoke test. Cheap to\n  // keep around.\n  if (pathname === \"/api/inventory/events/smoke\" && method === \"GET\") {\n    // SSE headers. The four important ones:\n    //   - Content-Type text/event-stream tells EventSource it's a stream\n    //   - Cache-Control no-cache prevents intermediate caches from\n    //     storing the stream\n    //   - Connection keep-alive is implicit on HTTP/1.1 but explicit\n    //     here for proxies that read headers verbatim\n    //   - X-Accel-Buffering: no is the nginx/Bifrost-specific signal\n    //     that disables response buffering; WITHOUT this the proxy\n    //     accumulates output and flushes only at close (the failure\n    //     mode this whole smoke test exists to detect)\n    res.writeHead(200, {\n      ...CORS_HEADERS,\n      \"Content-Type\": \"text/event-stream\",\n      \"Cache-Control\": \"no-cache, no-transform\",\n      \"Connection\": \"keep-alive\",\n      \"X-Accel-Buffering\": \"no\",\n    });\n\n    // Helper — SSE wire format: `id: ...\\nevent: ...\\ndata: ...\\n\\n`.\n    // Blank line terminates a message; EventSource then dispatches it.\n    let eventId = 0;\n    const send = (eventName: string, payload: Record<string, unknown>): void => {\n      eventId += 1;\n      const line =\n        `id: ${eventId}\\n` +\n        `event: ${eventName}\\n` +\n        `data: ${JSON.stringify(payload)}\\n\\n`;\n      try {\n        res.write(line);\n      } catch {\n        // socket closed mid-write — let the close handler clean up\n      }\n    };\n\n    // First event fires immediately so a successful connection is\n    // visible to the client even if no heartbeats have fired yet.\n    send(\"open\", { ts: Date.now(), msg: \"smoke stream opened\" });\n\n    // Heartbeat every 10 s. Two roles:\n    //   1. Proves the stream is still receiving server-side writes.\n    //   2. Keeps proxy idle-timeouts (nginx default 60 s) from killing\n    //      the connection on quiet streams.\n    const heartbeat = setInterval(() => {\n      send(\"tick\", { ts: Date.now(), eventId });\n    }, 10_000);\n\n    // Hard cap so a forgotten browser tab doesn't pin a worker forever.\n    // 5 min is long enough to confirm proxy behavior is stable across\n    // typical Bifrost idle-timeout windows.\n    const maxDurationMs = 5 * 60 * 1000;\n    const timeout = setTimeout(() => {\n      send(\"close\", { ts: Date.now(), reason: \"max-duration\" });\n      try { res.end(); } catch { /* ignore */ }\n    }, maxDurationMs);\n\n    // Client disconnect cleanup. `req.on('close')` fires when the\n    // browser/curl drops or the proxy times out. Without this, the\n    // interval keeps firing into a dead socket forever.\n    const cleanup = () => {\n      clearInterval(heartbeat);\n      clearTimeout(timeout);\n    };\n    req.on(\"close\", cleanup);\n    res.on(\"close\", cleanup);\n\n    return true;\n  }\n\n  if (pathname === \"/api/inventory/alerts\" && method === \"GET\") {"

> AGENT

Clean. The smoke endpoint is wired.

## What's shipped

**`GET /api/inventory/events/smoke`** — no auth, no payload, just a clean SSE stream:
- `open` event immediately on connect.
- `tick` event every 10 seconds, monotonically incrementing `id` field.
- Hard cap at 5 minutes with a `close` event, then `res.end()`.
- Cleans up `setInterval` + `setTimeout` on client disconnect (no socket leaks).
- Headers set explicitly: `Content-Type: text/event-stream`, `Cache-Control: no-cache, no-transform`, `Connection: keep-alive`, `X-Accel-Buffering: no`.

It stays installed permanently — even after Phase 2 ships, this is a useful "is SSE alive through the proxy" probe.

## How to verify

**Step 1 — deploy to dev Bifrost.** Push to `personal main` on `office-ops-engine`, then:

```
bifrost deploy --commit <full-sha>
```

(Standard rule from `office-ops-engine/CLAUDE.md` — always pass `--commit`. Need that explicit so we don't accidentally serve another project's image.)

**Step 2 — local sanity first** (while you wait for Bifrost, this catches dumb bugs):

```bash
npm run start:backend
# in another shell:
curl -N -H 'Accept: text/event-stream' http://localhost:4112/api/inventory/events/smoke
```

You should see:
```
id: 1
event: open
data: {"ts":1733649123456,"msg":"smoke stream opened"}

id: 2
event: tick
data: {"ts":1733649133456,"eventId":2}
```

…with each block appearing as it's written, not at the end. If local shows buffering, fix that first; no point testing Bifrost.

**Step 3 — through Bifrost** (the real test):

```bash
curl -N -H 'Accept: text/event-stream' \
  https://ops-engine-dev-330299.bifrost.saastack.site/api/inventory/events/smoke
```

Watch carefully for ~2-3 minutes.

## Three outcomes

**🟢 Pass:** First chunk arrives in <1 s, ticks flow every 10 s steadily, connection stays open past 60 s without dropping. SSE works through Bifrost — Phase 2 SSE work is **go**.

**🔴 Buffering failure:** Either no output for ~60 s then a big burst at the end, OR connection drops at exactly the proxy's idle timeout. Bifrost is buffering or has an aggressive timeout. SSE is **blocked** until either (a) Bifrost ops adds `proxy_buffering off;` and bumps `proxy_read_timeout` for this service, or (b) we serve the SSE endpoint through a different path that bypasses the buffering proxy.

**🟡 Intermittent:** Works for a minute, then connection drops. Likely a proxy idle-timeout that's longer than 10 s but shorter than what we hoped. Two mitigations possible — either tighten the heartbeat interval to stay under the timeout (cheap), or push for a longer timeout config (correct).

Once you've run it, tell me which of the three you saw and I'll plan the next step.

If you don't want to deploy yet and just want me to verify it works locally, I can run the smoke test against `npm run start:backend` — but **only the Bifrost-side test actually answers the go/no-go question**. Local pass is necessary but not sufficient.
